THE CENTRAL CHIT FUNDS ACT, 1982 &
THE A.P.CHIT FUNDS RULES, 2008

ABSTRACT 7
RIGHTS OF THE FOREMAN

1. To obtain chit amount in full at the first instalment or
at the instalment, specified in the chit agreement without de-
duction of any discount [Sec.21(1)(a)]

2. To the commission / remuneration not exceeding five
percent of the chit amount as may be fixed in the chit agree-
ment for meeting the expenses of running the chit
[Sec.21(1)(b)]

3. To interest or penalty, if any, payable by defaulting
subscribers and to

such other amounts as may be payable to the foreman
under the provisions of the chit agreement [Sec.21(1)(c)]

4. To receive and realise all subscriptions from the
subscribers and to distribute the prize amounts to the prized
subscribers [Sec.21(1)(d)]

5) To demand sufficient security from the prized
subscribers towards their future liability [Sec.21(1)(e)]

Explanation : - A security (movable property) shall be
deemed to be sufficient if its value exceeds by one third or if it
consists of immovable properties, whose value exceeds by
one and a half of the amount due from the prized subscriber

6. To substitute subscribers acceptable to him (foreman)
in place of defaulting subscribers [Sec.21(i)(f)]

7. To refer to the Registrar for arbitration under sec.64,
any dispute with regard to the value of the property
offered as security by the prized subscribers [Sec.21(2)]
Chit Fund Act, 1982 & A.P.Rulés, 2008

208

8. To the interest accrued on the foreman's security of
cash deposited in approved bank under sec.20(1)(a) [Para 6
of the judgment of Madras. High Court in W.P.No.6903/1984,
AIR 1993 Mad.(90)]

9. To the interest accruing on the deposits of the amount
of future subscriptions deducted from the prized amount -
under second proviso to section 22(1) [Second proviso to
section 22(4)] :

10. To remove defaulting non-prized subscribers in the pay-
ment of their subscriptions and removed in terms of the chit
agreement [Sec.28(1)]

11. To readmit the removed defaulting subscriber if he pays
the defaulted instalments with interest not exceeding twelve
percent per annum within seven days of the date of receipt of
notice of removal [Proviso to section 28(1) & Rule 63] and

12. To recognise transfer of chit by non-prized subscriber

provided the transferee is acceptable to the foreman [Secs.

35 & 36]

ABSTRACT 8

Foreman's duties and responsibilities

1. To conduct all draws/auctions on the due dates till
the last instalment as specified in the chit agreement

2. To receive and realize subscriptions from all sub-
scribers, to issue receipts in form xvi and to distribute the
prize amount to the prized subscribers [Sec.21(d)]

3. Torecord the minutes of proceedings of every draw/
auction in the minute book with all particulars specified in
section 17 and to file a true copy of the minutes with the
Registrar of Chits within twenty one days from the date of
draw/auction. [vide also abstract 9 infra and Secs.17, 18 &
Rule 17]

4. To pay the prize amount to the prized subscribers
on their furnishing sufficient security for the due payment of
future subscriptions [Sec.22(1)]

5. To distribute the dividend among the subscribers in
accordance with the Articles of the chit agreement
[Sec.22(6)]

6. To deposit in a separate identifiable account in an
approved bank specified in the chit agreement, the amount
contributed by the defaulting subscriber and the amount
realised from the. susbstituted subscriber less such
deduction as may be provided for in the chit agreement
and intimate the fact to the defaulting subscriber and the
Registrar [Sec.30(1)]

7. To deposit in a separate account in the approved
Chit Fund Act, 1982 & A.P.Rules, 2008

210

bank the prize amount remaining unpaid on the date of next
succeeding draw and to intimate in writing the fact of such
deposit to the prized subscriber and the Registrar [Sec. 22(2)]

8. To hold another draw if the prized subscriber fails to
collect the prize amount in respect of any instalment within a
period of two months from the date of the draw [Proviso
under section 22(2)]

9.» To intimate to the subscribers at the next succeeding
draw the’ deposit of the prize amount remaining unpaid
[Sec.22(3)] .

10. Not to appropriate to himself any amount in excess of
what he is entitled to under section’21(1)(b) or (c)[Sec.22(4)]

11. Not to admit any person as a subscriber in excess of
the number of tickets in any chit [section 22(5)]

12. To refund to the non-prized subscribers at the termi-
nation of chit their subscriptions unless otherwise provided for
in the Act or in the chit agreement [Sec.42]

13. To pay to the defaulting ‘subscribers who have been
substituted, their contributions subject to such deductions as
may be provided for in the chit agreement within fifteen days
from the date of termination of the chit [Se.30(3)]

14. To deposit in an approved bank mentioned in the chit
agreement the amount deducted towards future subscriptions
from the prized subscriber [Second proviso under. section .22]

15..To do all other acts that may be necessary for the due
and: proper. conduct: of the chit [Sec.21(g)]

ABSTRACT 9
Minutes Book - Maintenance

No particular form is prescribed for the maintenance of the
Minutes book (Rule 17) _

The proceedings of every minute pertaining to each draw-/
auction should contain the following particulars (Rule 17) :-

‘1. Date and hour when proceedings began and ended and
the place where the draw was held [Sec.17(2)(a)]

2. The number of the instalment of thé chit to which the
proceedings relate. [Sec.17(2)(b)]

3. The names of the subscribers present [Sec. 17(2)(c)]

4. The person or persons declared as prized subscriber
[Sec.17(2)(d)]

5. The amount of discount [Sec.17(2)(e)]

6. Full particulars regarding the disposal of the unpaid
prize amount, if any, in respect of any previous instalments..
[Sec.17(2)(f)]

7. Particulars of deposit, if any, of the prize amount. re-
maining unpaid on account of default of the prized subscriber,
made under Sec.22(2) since the date of the previous draw/
auction [Rule 17(1)(a)]

8. Particulars of money towards future subscriptions
deducted, if any, from the prize amount deposited in approved
Bank under second proviso to Sec.22(1)[Rule 17(1)(a)]

9. Particulars of deposit made in a separate identifiable
Chit Fund Act, 1982 & A.P.Rules, 2008
212
account in approved Bank of the contributions received from
the defaulting subscribers if any, less such deductions as may
be provided for in the chit agreement'since the date of the
previous draw/auction [Rule 17(1)(b)]

10. Particulars of consolidated payment of future subscrip-
tions realised from prized defaulting subscribers deposited in
approved bank since the date of the previous draw [Rule

17(1)(b)]

11. Paticulars of the amount withdrawn from the approved
Bank (Bank name to be specified) and the purpose for. which
the amount was withdrawn since the date of the previous
draw/auction [Rule 17(1)(c)]

12. How the prized subscriber was ascertained and the
particulars of his ticket and the prize amount [Rule 17(d)]

13. Full particulars of the commission/remuneration pay-
able to the foreman and the amount of dividend payable to
each subscriber [Rule 17(1)(e)]

14, Names of subscribers or their authorized agents who
bid at-the draw, their ticket numbers and their signatures [Rule
17(1)(f)]

15. Any other particulars that may be prescribed
[sec.17(2)(9)]

Penal provisions for contraventions (Section 76 & 77)

The foreman or whoever committing or abetting any of
the following offences, on conviction, is liable to be punished
with imprisonment upto two years or with fine upto
rupees five thousands or with both [Sec.76(1)]

SI.No. Nature of Contravention

1 Commencing or conducting chit without obtaining
previous sanction of the State Government or of
the authorised officer and without registration of
the chit [Sec.4(1)]

2 Issuing any notice, circular, prospectus, advertise-
ments etc.,inviting the public for enrolment to any
chit and without containing a statement that previ-
ous sanction required under Section 4 has been ob-
tained and the particulars of such sanction[Sec.5]

3 To commence or carry on chit business by any COM-
PANY on and after the date of commencement of
the Act without paid up capital of not less than ru-
pees one Lakh.[Sec.8(1)]

4 Failure to°create and maintain Reserve Fund by a
Company for carrying on chit business [Sec.8(3)]

5 Appropriation of amounts from the Reserve Fund
without prior approval of the Registrar [Sec.8(4)]

or appropriation of any chit amount without

ABSTRACT 14
Disputes and Arbitration [Secs.64 to 69 & Rules 45 to 57]

1) Section 64 of the Chit Funds Act, 1982 empowered
the Registrars of Chits to arbitrate disputes touching the man-
agement of any chit business between the parties to the chit
and the foreman. The Registrar's decision whether any
matter referred to him for his award is or is not.a dispute
arising out of the chit business is final [Sec.64(2)]. Civil Courts
are barred from entertaining suits or other proceedings in re-
spect of such disputes [Sec.64(3)].

2) Section 65 provides special periods of limitation not-
withstanding any thing contained in the Limitation Act, 1933.
The Registrar, in his discretion, may admit a dispute after the
expiry of the period of limtation if the applicant satisfies him
that the applicant had ” sufficient cause " for the delay.

’ Case Law : - What is " sufficient cause " has to be decided
on the merits of each case [AIR 2000 Mad.78]

3) Section 66 lays down that the Registrar shall settle the
dispute himself or refer it for disposal to his nominee. The
Registrar is also authorised to withdraw such disputes from
the nominee, settle the dispute himself or refer it again for
settlement to any other of his nominees and record the rea-
sons in writing for such withdrawal.

4) Section 67 refers to the procedure for settlement of
disputes and powers of the Registrar / nominee. They shall
have the same powers as are vested in a Civil Court viz

a) to summon and enforce the. attendance of persons and
exmine.them on. oath
Abstracts

. 227
b) to require the discovery and inspection of documents

c) to-receive evidence on affidavits

d) to requesition any public record or copies thereof from
any Court or office

e) to issue commissions for the examination of witnesses
or documents and on

f). any other matter which may be prescribed

5) The Registrar/nominee may also permit a legal
practioner to represent parties at the hearing of a dispute
[Sec.67(2)]. He may order any person who has acquired the
interest in the property to join as a party to the dispute and
his decision on the dispute shail be binding on such party as if
he were an original party to the dispute [Sec.67(3)(a)]. In
genuine cases he may also order that any other person be
substituted or added as parties to the dispute at any stage of
hearing on such terms as he thinks fit in genuine cases
[Sec.67(b)] and that the name of any party improperly joined
be struck off at any stage of the proceedings [Sec.67(c)].

6) The Registrar/nominee may direct conditional attach-
ment of any property before judgment if he is satisfied that a
party to such a dispute, with a malafide intention, is about to
dispose of the whole or any. part of his property or.is about to
remove the whole or any part of his property from the juris-
diction of the Registrar [Sec.68(1)]. The Registrar/nominee,
on conditional attachment of any property, shall issue a notice
to the parties concerned to furnish adequate security within a
specified period. If such security is not provided, the Regis-
trar/nominee may confirm the order and after the dispute is
decided, he may dispose off such property towards the claim
Chit Fund Act, 1982 & A.P.Rules, 2008
228 -
[Sec.68(2)]. The Registrar/nominee, in order to prevent:ends
of justice being defeated, may. make such interlocutory or-

ders pending the award as may appear to him just and con-
venient [Sec.68(4)] .

7) Section 69 enjoins that the Registrar/nominee make
an award after giving a reasonable oppurtunity to the parties
to the dispute to be heard. He is also empowered to give his
award on the expenses incurred ‘by the parties or fees and
expenses payable to the Registrar/nominee.

-8) Areference of a dispute under section 64 shall be made
to the Registrar in writing in Form’ XVII along with fee pre-
scribed under Article 12 of Table of fees. The Registrar may
call fora certified copy of the relevant records and such other
statements or records as may be required by him [Rule.45]°

9) The Registrar shall on the basis of the reference made
to him in Form XVII, record his decision together with the
reasons therefor whether he is or is not satisfied about the
existence of a dispute [Rule.46] ‘

10) The Registrar, if satisfied that there is a dispute shall
decide himself or refer it to his nominee for disposal
[Rule.47(1)] provided the parties concerned have complied
with the condition of payment of court fees specified in Rule
57 [Rule 47(2)]

11) Procedure for hearing and decision of disputes : -

(a) Rule 49 of the Rules prescribes the procedure for
hearing and decision on the dispute. The Registrar/nominee
has to pronounce the award in the open court, either at once
or as soon as may be practicable on some future day of which
due notice be given to the parties. Rule 49 does not contem-
 

Abstracts

229
plate communication of the decision or text of the award to
the disputant. The party has to apply and obtain a certified
copy of any order, judgment or award made by the Registrar/
nominee on payment of the fee prescribed under Article 19 of
the Table of fees[Rule 49(6)]

(b)The Registrar/nominee shall record the evidence of
the parties to the dispute and the witnesses in english or in the
official language of the state i.e Telugu. On the basis of the
evidence so recorded and on consideration of any documen-
tary evidance a decision shall be given in writing (vide also
above para)

(c) Where neither party appears at the time of hearing
the case may be dismissed for default [Rule 49(2)]

(d) Where the opponent appears and the disputant does
not appear the case shall be dismissed unless the opponent
admits the claim or the part thereof in which case order may
be passed against the opponent on such admission and where
only a part of the claim is admited, the case may be dismissed
in so far as it relates to the remainder [Rule 49(3)]

(e) Where the disputant appears and opponent does
not appear at the hearing and if the summons was found duly
served, ex-party orders may be passed. When the summons
is served by any officer of the Registrar/nominee, his report
of service shall be obtained on oath [Rule 49(4)]

(f) Each paty may be granted, ordinarly, not more than
two adjournments at his request. Further adjournments, at
the discretion of the Registrar/nominee, may be granted on
payment of such costs to the other side and such fee to the
Registrar/nominee as directed [Rule 49(5)] :

dispute requiring

i) the attendance of the parties to'the dispute and of wit-

ii) the production of all books and documents relating to
the matter in dispute :

notice, as the case may be, was served and the name and
address of the person, if any, identifying the person. served
and witnessing the delivery or tender of the summon or the
notice, as the case may be [Rule 50(3)] .

(b) The official issuing the summons/notice may exam-
ine the serving officer on oath or cause him to be so exam-
ined by the officer through whom it is served and may make
such further enquiry in the matter and shall either declare the
summons/notice as duly served or order it’ to be served in
such manner as he thinks fit [Rule 50(4)]

13) Investigation of claims and objections against any
attachment : - The Registrar/nominee shali investigate into
the claim or objection if any against any attachment of any
property and dispose it of on merits. But no such investigation
is necessary if the claim or objection is considered as
frivolous [Proviso under Rule 51] ,

fer of property : - The Registrar/nominee acting under sec-

an entry of such certificate to be made in the Record of Rights
[Rule 54]

17) Procedure for execution of awards : -

(a) The Registrar/nominee shall forward every order or
award passed by him to the foreman or to the party con-
cerned with instructions to initiate execution proceedings forth-
with according to the provisions of Section 71 [Rule 55(1)]
‘Chit Fund Act, 1982 & A.P.Rules, 2008

234

(b) If the amount due under the award is not
recovered, the foreman or the party as the case may bé shall
forward the award to the Registrar for execution along with
the required information and state whether he desires to
execute the award through a Civil Court or through Revenue
authorities [Rule 55(2)]. The Registrar shall forward the appli-
cation to the proper authority for execution alongwith a
certificte under section 71 for transfer » property and’a copy
of proclomation issued in form xviii under Rule 54 [Rule 55(3)].
Every order of the State Government passed in appeal under
section 70 shall also be executed in the same manner [Rule

him [Rule 57(3)(a)] :

237

(c) The Registrar/nominee is empowered to order the fees

and expenses of determining the dispute to be paid by the

foreman or by such party or parties to the dispute according

to the scale laid down by the-Registrar. after taking into
account the amount deposited as above [Rule 57(3)(b)]

(d) The Registrar may, by general or special order, specify
the scale of fee and expenses to.be paid to him or his nomi-
nee [Rule 57(3)(c)]

ARBITRATION PROCEEDINGS
PRINCIPLES
(Based on the lectures by Sri.S.Surendrababu Reddy, Additional Director
and Faculty, APIA, Hyderabad on 23rd and 24th October 2008
delivered during training classes to Chit Registrars etc. in A.P. on
“Principles of Natural Justice”)
1. Natural Justice .
> Nothing but dealing fairly with the people
> Important procedural safeguard against discrimination, or arbitrary or
undue use of administrative power
> To-apply just, fair and reasonable procedure in decision making process
by adrhinistrative bodies
> Right to fair hearing
> The rule against bias

2. Quasi-judicial
» The proceeding in question is similar to a judicial proceeding
> Administrative authority required to adjudicate upon the lisp
> Such authority will be regarded as acting in a quasijudicial manner

3. Hearing
> Hearing means a fair hearing
238

Chit Fund Act, 1982 & A.P.Rules, 2008

Hearing may be (i) oral or personal hearing or (ii) through written

* representation

Oral hearing may be required when there are complex and technical
questions or complicated questions of fact are involved and evidence
needs to be taken from witnesses

Hearing before administrative bodies can be of two types; quasi-judicial
and administrative

Quasi-judicial hearings are formal and require observance of principles
of natural justice and fair hearing

Administrative hearings are less formal though some fair procedure
must be followed

A reasonable opportunity must be given to the party to present his

case

Hearing

Hearing means a fair hearing

Notice

Opportunity to the person to present his case
Disclosure of materials

Opportunity to cross-examine witnesses
Legal representation

No one should be condemned unheard

5. Notice . :

»
»

Notice is the first and the most important step in the hearing procedure

Any proceedings taken without notice is invalid

6. Valid Notice

>
>

It must be adequate
It must fully mention all the grounds on which the action is proposed to

be taken against the concerned person
Abstracts

239

7, Adequate Notice

»
>

There is no set standard” a

A notice which does not give any facts and other particulars are
insufficient and inadequate

Service of notice is mandatory

For a notice to be adequate, it has to be served properly

All grounds on which the action is being taken must be communicated

Proceedings taken without adequate notice will be vitiated

8. Statutory Notice

>

b
>
»

Form prescribed by statute has to be complied with
Principles of natural justice apply
When particular mode of service is prescribed it has to be followed

Minor irregularities may be condoned if no prejudice is caused to the

party

9, Ex-party proceedings

»

If notice is served and the person concerned does not respond to it,
adjudicatory proceedings may proceed ex-party .
If proper cause is shown subsequently the proceedings should be

reopened

10. Disclosure of Materials

»

The materials relied upon by the adjudicatory body to take the decision
must be made available to the party
If the decision is taken without disclosing the material, it would deny

the party effective opportunity to meet the case against him

11. Receiving evidence

>

In an oral hearing, the adjudicating authority has to give opportunity to
240

Chit Fund Act, 1982 & A.P.Rules, 2008

the party to produce evidence in support of his case and to rebut
evidence against him :

Adjudicating authority can decide whether evidence is necessary and
relevant to the enquiry before it

No power to summon witnesses or compel production of documents in
the absence of statutory provision :

12. Cross-examination

»

Where opportunity of cross examination is to be given depends upon
the facts and circumstances of the case

Generally in an oral hearing, if witnesses are examined against a person,
he haé a right to cross-examine the witnesses and denial of this right
might vitiate the proceedings

There may be situations when it may not be expedient to allow cross-
examination. For example, when there is threat to the life of witness
or property of the witnesses or where the identity of the witness is to
be kept confidential

13. Reasoned Decisions

>
7

Reasons introduce clarity and transparency in administration
Minimizes arbitrariness and promotes public confidence in administrative
process

It is mandatory to give a reasoned order

An order without reasons deprives the party of the right of appeal or

revision

14. Reasoned Order

>
»
>

Reasons must be adequate
A speaking order is one that speaks the mind of the adjudicating authority
Statute may exclude the requirement to give reasons
Abstracts /
241
15. Bias
> Bias may be defined as a preconceived opinion or a predisposition or
pre-determination to decide a case or an issue in a particular manner
so much so that such pre-disposition does not leave the mind open to
conviction. It is a condition of mind, which sways the judgments and
renders the judge unable to exercise impartiality ina particular case
[(1998) 5 SCC 5]
» Justice must not only be done but be seen to have been done

16. Types of Bias
> Pecuniary bias
> Personal bias
> Policy bias

17. Pecuniary bias
» This bias arises when the adjudicating authority has some pecuniary
interest in the outcome of the case before him
> Even an insignificant interest in the subject. matter of the case
disqualifies a person from acting as judge

18. Personal Bias

> Personal bias arises when one of the parties to the. case is related to~
the adjudicating authority either as a family member, friend or through
some business or professional relationship

> The test is whether there is a real likelihood of bias as against a_
reasonable suspicion of bias -

> Another test is that the justice must not only be done but appear to
have been done

» Actual bias is not necessary to disqualify a person from acting as
adjudicator, If there is a real likelyhood of bias then also he may be

disqualifed
Chit Fund Act, 1982 & A.P.Rules, 2008

242
19. Policy or official Bias

» Policy or official bias arises where the adjudicatory body is part of the

same department against which the claim is made

» It has to be demonstrated that the adjudicating authority has a strong
‘ or blatant bids towards the department or the policy underchallenge or
“was actually involved in formulating the policy under challenge
> Mere official or policy bias does not disqualify a person as adjudicator.

20. Exclusion of Bias
» Necessity is an exception to rule against bias. An adjudicator'subject
to disqualification may have to adjudicate in situations where
(1) there is no one else competent to adjudicate the’case or
(2) a quorum cannot be constituted without him or

(3) no other competent tribunal can be constituted

21.. Waiver of Bias
> Waiver may. be inferred when the party.
(1). knows that the adjudicator is disqualified because of bias
(2) knows that he has a right to object thereto
(3) he acquiesced in the proceedings by failing to take an objection at
the earliest opportunity

22. EXAMINATION OF WITNESSES ON OATH
> "Ido swear in the name of God that I shall state the truth, the
whole truth and nothing but truth"

“ BGS dot iitreso Bh wow Nash Bark. aso Bays”

Qe Pk WON

ELUCIDATION

The stamp duty on duplicate of the chit agreement is
based on the duty payable and paid on the original chit agree-
ment. If the duty payable and paid:on the original chit agree-
ment does not exceed rupees ten, the duty payable on the
duplicate is the same i.e rupees ten only.

In any other case, that is when the duty payable and paid
on the original chit agreement exceeds rupees ten, the duty
on the duplicate chit agreement payable shall be rupees twenty
(fixed) irrespective of the chit value and the stamp duty
payable and paid for the original chit agreement. Art. 22 of
Schedule 1A is to be read with section 16 of the Indian
stamp Act 1899 extracted below.
Chit Fund Act, 1982 & A.P.Rules, 2008

254
SECTION 16

"Denoting stamp duty : Where the duty with which an
instrument is chargeable, or its exemption from duty. de-
pends in any manner upon the duty actually paid in respect of
another instrument, the payment of such last mentioned duty
shall, if application is made in writing to the Collector [(sub
Registrars/Registrars of the Registration and stamps depart-
ment are appointed as “collector” under section 16 of the
stamp Act, 1899 (B.P 320 dt 09/12/1899)] for that purpose
and on production of both the instruments be denoted upon
such first mentioned instrument, by endorsement, under the
hand of the collector or in such other manner (if any) as the
State Government may by rule prescribe"

ELUCIDATION

‘This section requires making of an application (Vide
Proforma below) to. the Sub Registrar or the Registrar, along
with the chit agreement and its duplicate. The application should
bear a court fee stamp of rupee one under the Court Fees
Act. The Sub registrar/Registrar is empowered to denote the
duty paid in respect of the original chit. agreement on its
duplicate. Then only the duplicate will be considered as duly
stamped. . : ,

Chit Fund business organisations stand to gain and
save Rs.30/- (Thirty) on EACH DUPLICATE of the chit
agreement if they avail and comply with the provisons
of Section 16 and the cumulative benefit will be
substantial . “
